Well, I... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I mean, I still know what I like. It's just... maybe I haven't been quite as active with my hobbies lately.

Well, I guess... maybe a little bit less. I mean, I'm definitely not starving myself or anything like that. I still sit down for meals.

I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I still get up, go to the floor, and get things done. I try to stay positive and not let things get to me. I don't want you to think I'm some sort of nervous wreck or anything. I handle things pretty well, mostly. Is that... does that answer your question okay, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I mean, I still know what I like. I mean, I might be waking up a little earlier than I used to, but I figure that's just part of getting older, right? I still have plenty of energy for work. I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself. That sounds pretty typical, doesn't it, Doctor? Well, yeah, I suppose it has been every day. It's been right about two weeks now. That's... well, that's right when my daughter actually left Georgia for the military. 

So this whole change in me, I guess you could call it, has been going on since then. It lines up perfectly with her leaving, so I'm sure it's just a temporary adjustment period. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, maybe a little bit, but nothing I can't handle, you know? It's just... at the distribution center, I'll be looking at the shift schedule or one of the routing sheets, and... well, my mind just doesn't want to lock in the way it usually does. 

I'll find myself staring at it for a minute, and then suddenly a little thing, like a minor staffing call, feels like a much bigger deal than it ought to be. But I'm still getting everything done, I promise! I just have to double-check my work a little more than usual. That's a pretty normal reaction when you're a bit stressed and tired, right? Just a little brain fog? I'm sure I'll be sharp as a tack again once I get used to my daughter being away.

Well, I... I guess some folks at the distribution center have made a couple of comments. But you know how people are, always making a fuss over nothing!

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hopeless? Oh, no, sir. I wouldn't use a strong word like that. 

I mean, sure, I've been feeling a little... a little drained and worried, like we talked about, but I always try to look on the bright side. You have to keep pushing forward, right? I mean, it's a good thing she's doing. I just... maybe I haven't handled the quiet house as well as I thought I would. I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it. Does that sound like the sort of thing that could cause this, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I've been worrying a lot. I mean, everybody worries a little bit, right? Especially with a kid in the military now, it's just natural to think about them and hope they're doing okay. \n\nBut it's not like I'm sitting around wringing my hands or letting it get in the way of my work. I still get up, go to the floor, and get things done. I try to stay positive and not let things get to me. I don't want you to think I'm some sort of nervous wreck or anything. I handle things pretty well, mostly. Is that... does that answer your question okay, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I mean, I still know what I like. It's just... maybe I haven't been quite as active with my hobbies lately. \n\nI usually love messing around with my tomato plants out back, or sanding some wood down at my workbench. And I always try to catch the Falcons games, or stay after church on Sundays to talk with folks. But these past couple of weeks... I don't know, I just look at the garden or my tools, and it almost feels like they belong to somebody else. I just haven't had the drive to pick them up. \n\nI'm sure it's just a temporary thing, you know? Just a little phase while I get used to the house being so quiet. I'm not trying to be lazy or anything, I promise. I'm sure I'll get back to it all soon. Does that make sense, Doctor? Is it normal to take a little break from things like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... sleep has been pretty normal, I think. I mean, I might be waking up a little earlier than I used to, but I figure that's just part of getting older, right? You don't need as much sleep when you hit my age. \n\nI'm definitely not staying up all night or anything crazy like that. I still go to bed at a decent hour. Maybe I toss and turn a little bit, just thinking about my daughter and hoping she's doing alright at basic training, but... I wouldn't say I'm sleeping less in a bad way. I still have plenty of energy for work. I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself. That sounds pretty typical, doesn't it, Doctor? Just normal aging and a little bit of dad-worry?\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... maybe a little bit less. I mean, I'm definitely not starving myself or anything like that. I still sit down for meals. \n\nIt's just that, lately, most days I kind of have to make myself eat a few bites. Even the stuff I normally really like, you know, whether it's something I grab at the warehouse or a nice dinner at home... it just sort of sits there on the plate until I end up throwing it out. I just don't have much of a taste for it right now. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just my stomach adjusting to things, or maybe just part of getting a little older. I'm staying hydrated, though, I promise. I don't want you to worry that I'm neglecting my health. That happens sometimes, right, Doctor? Just a little dip in appetite?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't use a strong word like worthless. I mean, I still go in and run things at the warehouse, I'm still doing my job and pulling my weight. \n\nBut... I guess I have been a little hard on myself lately. It's just, with my daughter leaving for basic training and all... I keep going over it in my head. I just keep telling myself I must have failed her somehow. Like, maybe a better father would have done something different, you know? Set things up better for her so she didn't have to go off like that. \n\nBut I know that's probably just me overthinking things. I'm not sitting around hating myself or anything like that, I promise. I just want to be a good dad. It's pretty normal to second-guess yourself when your kid leaves the nest, right, Doctor? Every parent feels a little guilty sometimes, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to kill yourself in the past?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. No, I've never... I would never actually try to do anything to hurt myself. I promise you that. I know I have responsibilities, and I wouldn't do that to my family. \n\nBut, um... well, I guess I should just be completely honest with you, since we're talking about it. The whole reason I actually came in for this appointment is... well, lately I keep having these thoughts. Just... thoughts that maybe my family might be better off if I just didn't wake up. \n\nIt's really embarrassing to admit that out loud, and I want you to know I'm definitely not planning to do anything! It just... it scared me a little bit, having those kinds of things popping into my head. It spooked me enough that I actually pulled my supervisor aside at the warehouse and told him about it, and that's how I ended up here. I really want to do the responsible thing and take care of this. It was good that I told him and came in to see you, wasn't it, Doctor? I'm not going crazy or anything, right? Just a little overwhelmed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most days lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say sad, exactly. I mean, I'm not sitting around crying all day or anything like that. I still get up, get dressed, go to the warehouse... I try to keep a smile on my face for the guys. \n\nI guess I've just been feeling a little... flat, maybe? Like the color is sort of washed out of things right now. But I'm sure that's just because I've been tired and, you know, with everything going on. It's not like a deep, dark depression or anything, I promise. Just a little rut, I think. \n\nEveryone gets a little down sometimes, right, Doctor? It's just a normal part of life, isn't it? I'm sure it'll pass once I get my energy back.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings and thoughts been present every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I suppose it has been every day. It's been right about two weeks now. That's... well, that's right when my daughter actually left Georgia for the military. \n\nSo this whole change in me, I guess you could call it, has been going on since then. It lines up perfectly with her leaving, so I'm sure it's just a temporary adjustment period. It's pretty normal to feel a bit off every day when you go through a big family change like that, right, Doc? I mean, any father would be a little thrown off his game for a couple of weeks under those circumstances, wouldn't he?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Well, maybe a little bit, but nothing I can't handle, you know? It's just... at the distribution center, I'll be looking at the shift schedule or one of the routing sheets, and... well, my mind just doesn't want to lock in the way it usually does. \n\nI'll find myself staring at it for a minute, and then suddenly a little thing, like a minor staffing call, feels like a much bigger deal than it ought to be. But I'm still getting everything done, I promise! I just have to double-check my work a little more than usual. That's a pretty normal reaction when you're a bit stressed and tired, right? Just a little brain fog? I'm sure I'll be sharp as a tack again once I get used to my daughter being away.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two weeks, have you ever had another period like this in your life?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it hasn't just been these past two weeks. I mean, I've had these... these low stretches sort of coming and going for maybe about a year now? \n\nBut they were never a big deal, really! Just a day here or there where I felt a little sluggish. I always pushed through it at the warehouse. It's just... this time, with my daughter leaving, it's definitely hit me the hardest. It's just a bit tougher to shake off right now. But it's nothing too serious, I'm sure of it. It's pretty common for people my age to have a slightly off year before a big life event, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Before these past two weeks, was there ever a time when you felt this low nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, no, sir... I don't think so. Not for two whole weeks straight, no. \n\nLike I was saying, before this, it was mostly just a day or two here and there where I felt a little slow. I'd always bounce right back and get to work. I never let it drag on day after day like this before. I always kept it together, you know? I'd never let it get in the way for a whole two weeks. \n\nSo... since this is really the very first time it's ever stuck around this long, that probably just proves it's all tied to my daughter moving away, right? Because I haven't had a two-week stretch like this before, that's gotta be a pretty good sign that I'm basically doing okay overall, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess some folks at the distribution center have made a couple of comments. But you know how people are, always making a fuss over nothing! \n\nThey keep asking if I'm coming down with something because I guess I'm... well, I'm walking a bit slower than my usual pace. And sometimes it takes me a second to answer a simple question about the manifests. It just feels like... like my body is moving through mud, you know? Just a little heavy. \n\nBut I'm still getting my steps in on the floor! I'm just taking my time, being careful. It's not like I'm holding up the whole line or anything. It's perfectly normal to slow down a step or two when you're feeling a bit drained, right, Doctor? I'm sure I'm not the only one who gets a little sluggish when they're stressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say \"guilty,\" exactly. That's a strong word. I mean, sure, I... I guess I've been doing a little bit of thinking lately. Just, you know, normal father stuff. \n\nWith my daughter packing up to leave, I just... I keep going over things in my head. Wondering if I... if maybe I failed her somehow? Like, maybe if I was a better father, I would have done things differently, or... or she wouldn't be in such a rush to get out on her own. \n\nBut I know every parent second-guesses themselves, right? It's just a sign that I care! I'm sure it's completely normal to want to be the best provider you can be, and to worry you fell a little short. That's just part of being a good dad, isn't it, Doctor? I don't think it means anything is wrong with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? Oh, no, sir. I wouldn't use a strong word like that. \n\nI mean, sure, I've been feeling a little... a little drained and worried, like we talked about, but I always try to look on the bright side. You have to keep pushing forward, right? I've still got my job at the center, and... and I know my daughter is going to do just fine out there on her own. I haven't given up or anything drastic like that. \n\nAs long as a man keeps trying and putting one foot in front of the other, there's always hope, wouldn't you agree? I think the fact that I'm still trying my best shows I'm still doing okay, deep down... right, Doctor? I'm definitely not hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to end your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, I wouldn't say I've made... plans. That sounds so... so final. I wouldn't do that to my family. \n\nBut... well, I guess I should be honest since you're trying to help. I... I did come in today because... well, I've just been having these thoughts lately. Just passing thoughts, really! But... I keep catching myself thinking that maybe... maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up tomorrow. Like maybe my daughter and everyone would just be better off without me dragging them down. \n\nIt actually scared me a bit, to be honest. I even mentioned it to my supervisor at the center, which is why I'm here. But it's just thoughts, Doctor. I wouldn't actually do anything. It's normal to get a little overwhelmed and think silly things when you're under a lot of pressure, right? I'm sure you hear that from a lot of hardworking folks... right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Tired? Um... well, I suppose I'm a little tired, but nothing unusual, really. I mean, I'm 57, right? You can't expect a guy my age to have the same pep in his step as a twenty-year-old. \n\nI still get up and go to work every day. Managing the center takes a lot out of a person, and I like to think I work hard for my paycheck. So, sure, by the time I get home, I'm ready for the recliner. But that's just the sign of an honest day's work, don't you think? It's perfectly normal to feel a little worn out when you're carrying a lot of responsibility... isn't it, Doctor? I'm definitely not letting it stop me from doing what needs to be done."
